# Add Makefile target for syntax sample testing

## User

Continuing work from thread T-019b239e-0f2f-71ce-b793-63a784c6aa4f. When you lack specific information you can use read_thread to get it.

I just finished implementing syntax highlighting for all Phase 3, 4, and 5 languages as outlined in the roadmap. This involved:
- Adding new tree-sitter grammar dependencies to Cargo.toml.
- Updating the LanguageId enum in src/syntax/languages.rs, including its from_extension() and display_name() methods.
- Creating new .scm query files in the queries/ directory for TypeScript, JSON, and TOML.
- Using the built-in HIGHLIGHTS_QUERY or HIGHLIGHT_QUERY for Python, Go, PHP, C, C++, Java, and Bash.
- Registering all new languages in the ParserState::init_language() function in src/syntax/parser.rs.
- Adding dedicated query compilation tests and basic parsing tests for each new language in src/syntax/parser.rs.
- Upgrading the core tree-sitter dependency from 0.24 to 0.25 to resolve ABI compatibility issues with newer grammar crates.
- Updating docs/ROADMAP.md and docs/CHANGELOG.md to reflect these changes.
- I also created comprehensive sample files for each of the 17 supported languages in the samples/syntax/ directory.

The current syntax highlighting system runs in a background worker thread with a 30ms debounce. It uses tree-sitter for parsing and query-based highlighting. Queries are embedded using include_str!().

For the next task, I need to add a Makefile target to open all these sample files for manual testing.

1) TL;DR

- `SplitContainer.min_sizes` being unused is not a crash risk, just an unimplemented feature / design debt.

## 1. Critical issues

### 1.1 Hardcoded gutter width (`sync_all_viewports`)

Relevant code (current):

```rust
pub fn sync_all_viewports(&mut self, line_height: usize, char_width: f32) {
    // ...
    for (editor_ids, width, height) in group_info {
        // visible lines (ok)
        let visible_lines = if line_height > 0 {
            (height as usize) / line_height
        } else {
            25
        };

        // visible columns
        // Account for gutter/line numbers (estimate ~50px)
        let gutter_width = 50.0;
        let available_width = (width - gutter_width).max(0.0);
        let visible_columns = if char_width > 0.0 {
            (available_width / char_width).floor() as usize
        } else {
            80
        };

        // ...
    }
}
```

Compare with geometry helpers:

```rust
pub fn compute_visible_columns(window_width: u32, char_width: f32) -> usize {
    if char_width <= 0.0 {
        return 80;
    }
    let text_x = text_start_x(char_width).round();
    ((window_width as f32 - text_x) / char_width).floor() as usize
}
```

And `text_start_x` is the canonical left edge of the text area (re‑exported from the model).

**Is the issue real?**

Yes, but it’s a mild UI correctness issue, not a crash:

- The rendering code uses `text_start_x(char_width)` / `gutter_border_x(char_width)` for actual pixel positions (see `geometry.rs` and various uses of `text_start_x` and `gutter_border_x` in the view layer).
- The viewport sizing here assumes a *fixed* 50px gutter rather than `text_start_x(char_width)`, which is font/scale‑aware and may be ~57–67px as the review notes.

Consequences:

- The editor’s `visible_columns` can be off by roughly 0.5–1.5 columns depending on DPI/font metrics.
- That manifests as:
  - slight horizontal over/under‑scroll,
  - last column sometimes partially clipped or an extra blank column on the right.

No undefined behavior, no panics—just subtle mismatch between viewport model and actual drawing.

**Is the suggested fix correct?**

Yes. The consistent fix is:

```rust
use token::model::text_start_x; // or appropriate path in your model crate

let gutter_width = text_start_x(char_width);
let available_width = (width - gutter_width).max(0.0);
let visible_columns = if char_width > 0.0 {
    (available_width / char_width).floor() as usize
} else {
    80
};
```

This matches `geometry::compute_visible_columns` logic and keeps model and view in sync.

Note: `text_start_x` is defined in the model (re‑exported by `view::geometry`), so using it here does not introduce a view→model layering violation.

**Priority**

- **Priority: M‑ (low/medium)**
  - Worth fixing because it keeps the viewport logic consistent across the app and avoids “mysterious” off‑by‑one column behavior.
  - Not urgent: no crashes or data corruption.

**Effort:** **S** (< 1h).

---

### 1.2 `SplitContainer.min_sizes` not enforced

Current `SplitContainer`:

```rust
pub struct SplitContainer {
    pub direction: SplitDirection,
    pub children: Vec<LayoutNode>,
    /// Proportional sizes (0.0 to 1.0, must sum to 1.0)
    pub ratios: Vec<f32>,
    /// Minimum size in pixels for each child
    pub min_sizes: Vec<f32>,
}
```

Current layout computation ignores `min_sizes`:

```rust
fn compute_layout_node(&mut self, node: &LayoutNode, rect: Rect, splitters: &mut Vec<SplitterBar>) {
    match node {
        LayoutNode::Group(group_id) => { /* ... */ }
        LayoutNode::Split(container) => {
            let children = &container.children;
            let ratios = &container.ratios;

            // ...
            let total_size = match container.direction {
                SplitDirection::Horizontal => rect.width,
                SplitDirection::Vertical => rect.height,
            };

            for (i, child) in children.iter().enumerate() {
                let ratio = ratios.get(i).copied().unwrap_or(1.0 / children.len() as f32);
                let child_size = total_size * ratio;

                let child_rect = match container.direction { /* ... */ };

                // ...
                self.compute_layout_node(child, child_rect, splitters);
                offset += child_size;
            }
        }
    }
}
```

**Is the issue real?**

- Yes in the sense that `min_sizes` is currently unused; it does *nothing* during layout.
- But **it is not a crash risk** in the code shown:
  - Child sizes can become very small (even 0), but:
    - `group_content_rect` clamps to `max(0.0)`.
    - Viewport sizing does arithmetic like `height as usize / line_height` and `(width - gutter_width).max(0.0)`—safe with 0.
    - Rendering operations are all bounds-checked Rust code; 0‑sized rects simply render nothing.

So this is an **incomplete feature / design debt**, not a safety issue.

If elsewhere you clamp splitter dragging using `min_sizes` *before* recomputing `ratios`, then ignoring `min_sizes` here is less of a problem; but from this file alone, layout respects only `ratios`.

**Is the suggested fix (enforce `min_sizes` in `compute_layout_node`) correct?**

Conceptually yes (you *should* enforce `min_sizes` somewhere), but:

- The right place might be:
  - Where you update `ratios` during splitter drag,
  - Or here in `compute_layout_node` by adjusting child sizes and re‑normalizing `ratios`.
- Doing it correctly needs a bit of care:
  - Enforcing all `min_sizes[i]` while ensuring total size still sums to `rect.width`/`rect.height`.
  - Handling cases where the sum of all `min_sizes` > available size (need a fallback: clamp, redistribute, or disable further shrinking).

So “just enforce min_sizes in `compute_layout_node`” is underspecified and easy to get subtly wrong.

**Priority**

- **Priority: L‑ (low, but potentially UX‑nice)**
  - Only matters when users drag splitters to extremes; they might accidentally create nearly invisible groups.
  - No evidence of crash or serious bug from this alone.

**Effort (for a robust implementation):** **M** (1–3h), due to ratio rebalancing and edge‑case handling.

I’d treat this as a future enhancement rather than something to rush.

---

### 1.3 Alleged unsafe pointer dereference (editor_area.rs:276‑280)

The provided `editor_area.rs` around the only relevant mid‑file logic:

```rust
pub fn sync_all_viewports(&mut self, line_height: usize, char_width: f32) {
    // Collect group rects and their editor IDs
    let group_info: Vec<(Vec<EditorId>, f32, f32)> = self
        .groups
        .values()
        .map(|group| {
            let editor_ids: Vec<EditorId> = group.tabs.iter().map(|t| t.editor_id).collect();
            (editor_ids, group.rect.width, group.rect.height)
        })
        .collect();

    // Update each editor's viewport based on its group's dimensions
    for (editor_ids, width, height) in group_info {
        // ...
        for editor_id in editor_ids {
            if let Some(editor) = self.editors.get_mut(&editor_id) {
                editor.resize_viewport(visible_lines, visible_columns);
            }
        }
    }
}
```

- There is **no `unsafe` block** in this region.
- No raw pointers are taken from the `HashMap`s here.
- The only data leaving the `groups` `HashMap` is copied `EditorId`s and `f32` dimensions into a `Vec`; this is safe regardless of map reallocation.
- The subsequent mutation is on a different `HashMap` (`self.editors`), so even iterating `self.groups` here would be legal; but we’re not even doing that because we fully collect first.

**Conclusion**

- The “unsafe pointer dereference because HashMap may reallocate” finding does **not** apply to the code you’ve shown.
- It is almost certainly **outdated** (maybe from a prior version where someone held `&mut` pointers into the map or raw pointers) or simply a mistaken analysis.

**Priority**

- **No issue to fix.**
- If you want to be thorough, you can quickly `grep` for `unsafe` in `editor_area.rs` and confirm there’s no other block near the cited lines, but based on the provided code this is a **false positive**.

**Effort:** N/A.

## 2. Dead code assessment

### 2.1 `Renderer` getters in `view/mod.rs`

Dead‑code‑allowed methods:

```rust
impl Renderer {
    pub fn char_width(&self) -> f32 { self.char_width }

    #[allow(dead_code)]
    pub fn font(&self) -> &Font { &self.font }

    #[allow(dead_code)]
    pub fn font_size(&self) -> f32 { self.font_size }

    #[allow(dead_code)]
    pub fn line_height(&self) -> usize {
        self.line_metrics.new_line_size.ceil() as usize
    }

    #[allow(dead_code)]
    pub fn ascent(&self) -> f32 { self.line_metrics.ascent }

    #[allow(dead_code)]
    pub fn line_metrics(&self) -> &LineMetrics { &self.line_metrics }

    #[allow(dead_code)]
    pub fn glyph_cache_mut(&mut self) -> &mut GlyphCache { &mut self.glyph_cache }

    #[allow(dead_code)]
    pub fn dimensions(&self) -> (u32, u32) {
        (self.width, self.height)
    }
}
```

Observations:

- These are all straightforward accessors to internals of `Renderer`.
- They are **public instance methods**, not free functions; that suggests they’re part of the renderer’s public API surface, intended for either:
  - external consumers (e.g., main event loop, input handling, or tests),
  - or future geometry refactors (calling `renderer.line_height()` et al. instead of recomputing).
- They’re already explicitly silenced with `#[allow(dead_code)]`, so the current authors knew they were unused and opted to keep them.

Without the rest of the crate, we can’t mechanically prove they’re unused, but:

- `char_width` is clearly used.
- It’s plausible the others are *currently* unused but are:
  - useful for tests or debugging,
  - or intended to support future layout code that uses renderer metrics.

**Recommendation**

- If you value a minimal API and there’s *no* concrete near‑term use for these, you can remove the ones you don’t foresee using:
  - I’d keep at least `line_height()` and `dimensions()`—they’re commonly useful for layout and status bar sizing.
- Given they’re simple getters and already suppressed, the **cost of keeping them is tiny**.

**Priority**

- **Priority: L** (style/cleanup only).
- **Effort:** **S** to remove or keep as is.

My bias under “simplicity‑first” but also “don’t spend time on non‑issues”: **keep them** until they actively get in your way or you refactor the layout to consume them properly (see “advanced path” below).

---

### 2.2 `compute_visible_lines`, `compute_visible_columns` and other helpers in `geometry.rs`

Marked dead-code:

```rust
#[allow(dead_code)]
pub fn compute_visible_lines(window_height: u32, line_height: usize, status_bar_h: usize) -> usize {
    if line_height == 0 {
        return 25;
    }
    let available = (window_height as usize).saturating_sub(status_bar_h);
    available / line_height
}

#[allow(dead_code)]
pub fn compute_visible_columns(window_width: u32, char_width: f32) -> usize {
    if char_width <= 0.0 {
        return 80;
    }
    let text_x = text_start_x(char_width).round();
    ((window_width as f32 - text_x) / char_width).floor() as usize
}
```

Plus:

```rust
#[inline]
#[allow(dead_code)]
pub fn is_in_tab_bar(y: f64) -> bool { /* ... */ }

#[allow(dead_code)]
pub fn pixel_to_cursor_in_group(/* ... */) -> (usize, usize) { /* ... */ }

#[inline]
#[allow(dead_code)]
pub fn group_gutter_rect(/* ... */) -> Rect { /* ... */ }

#[inline]
#[allow(dead_code)]
pub fn group_text_area_rect(/* ... */) -> Rect { /* ... */ }
```

Note:

- These helpers encapsulate core layout math used elsewhere in prose:
  - `compute_visible_columns` directly mirrors the logic we discussed for `sync_all_viewports` and correctly uses `text_start_x(char_width)`.
  - `group_gutter_rect` / `group_text_area_rect` are the rect versions of gutter/text geometry.
- Many of them are exercised in the `#[cfg(test)]` module at the bottom:
  - `compute_visible_lines`, `compute_visible_columns`, `group_content_rect`, etc. are used in tests, so they are **not actually unreferenced** across the crate; Rust’s `dead_code` lint is about non-test usage, which is why `#[allow(dead_code)]` was added.

**Are these placeholders or actually dead?**

- They’re not pure placeholders: they encode the “single source of truth” geometry logic for various layout aspects and are already used in tests.
- Some are likely remnants of the single‑pane architecture and may be less central in the new split view, but they still represent cleaned‑up, test‑backed helpers that you *should* be using instead of hand-coded constants like `50.0`.

**Recommendation**

- **Do not delete them** right now. Instead:
  - Where appropriate, refactor callers to *use* these helpers to reduce duplicated math (e.g., `sync_all_viewports` → use the `compute_visible_columns` pattern).
  - Keep them as the canonical geometry layer; this is especially valuable for future correctness and testability.

**Priority**

- **Priority: L** (nice to refactor to use them; not harmful to keep).
- **Effort:** **S–M** if you refactor callers to use them consistently; **0** if you simply leave them.

---

## 3. False positives / overstated findings

Summarizing which review points are off or overstated:

1. **Unsafe pointer dereference in `editor_area.rs`**
   - **Status:** **False positive/outdated.**
   - No `unsafe` present in the provided code near the cited lines; no raw pointers from `HashMap`s; logic is safe.

2. **`min_sizes` leading to crash risk**
   - **Status:** Overstated.
   - Real issue: `min_sizes` is currently unused.
   - Impact: UX only (panes can be made tiny/0px). No clear crash or UB from the shown code.

3. **Hardcoded gutter width**
   - **Status:** Real issue, but *severity overstated if described as “viewport calculation is broken”*.
   - The viewport is slightly misaligned; behavior is mostly okay but not pixel‑perfect.
   - Fix is correct and should be applied, but not urgent.

---

## 4. Recommended approach (simple path)

Ordered by actual impact/benefit:

1. **Fix the gutter width/visible columns calculation** (**Priority M‑, Effort S**)
   - Replace `gutter_width = 50.0` in `sync_all_viewports` with:

     ```rust
     use token::model::text_start_x; // correct path for your crate

     let gutter_width = text_start_x(char_width);
     let available_width = (width - gutter_width).max(0.0);
     let visible_columns = if char_width > 0.0 {
         (available_width / char_width).floor() as usize
     } else {
         80
     };
     ```

   - This keeps viewport sizing consistent with your geometry helpers and rendering.

2. **Leave `min_sizes` as a known TODO** (**Priority L‑**)
   - Optionally add a short comment in `SplitContainer` or `compute_layout_node`:

     ```rust
     // TODO: enforce min_sizes either here or in splitter drag logic
     // to prevent panes from being shrunk below a usable size.
     ```

   - Track enforcement as a future UX improvement issue rather than a bugfix.

3. **Ignore the unsafe-pointer warning** (**Priority: none**)
   - Confirm via quick search that there’s no `unsafe` in `editor_area.rs`.
   - Close that review comment as obsolete.

4. **Keep “dead code” helpers** (**Priority L, Effort S or 0**)
   - Keep `Renderer` getters and `geometry` helpers for now; they’re low‑cost and useful abstraction points.
   - Later, either:
     - Refactor callers to use them, or
     - Remove the ones that remain truly unused after refactoring.

---

## 5. Rationale and trade‑offs

- **Simplest fix with real benefit:** Aligning gutter/visible columns with `text_start_x` removes a class of subtle layout bugs and keeps the model and view in sync. It’s a low‑risk, high‑confidence change.
- **Avoid premature complexity:** Enforcing `min_sizes` correctly requires a bit of nontrivial math and edge‑case handling. There’s no immediate correctness/safety pressure to do that now; better to implement it once with a clear design.
- **Not chasing ghosts:** Spending time on an unsafe issue that is not present wastes reviewer and developer cycles; discarding that finding is the pragmatic move.
- **Dead code vs future refactors:** The geometry helpers are exactly the kind of “single source of truth” code that prevent layout bugs like the hardcoded 50px. Deleting them now makes it harder to clean up layout logic later.

---

## 6. Risks and guardrails

- When changing `sync_all_viewports`, verify:
  - Split view with multiple groups on different monitor DPIs still behaves sensibly.
  - Horizontal scrolling doesn’t suddenly move by 1 column compared to previous behavior (or accept that as a one‑time correction).
- If you later implement `min_sizes`:
  - Guard against `sum(min_sizes) > total_size` and define a clear behavior (e.g., allow overflow but clamp to equal splits, or enforce a coarser minimum).
  - Add tests for extreme splitter positions to avoid regressions.

---

## 7. When to consider the advanced path

Consider a more advanced layout/viewport refactor if you observe:

- Frequent confusion or bug reports about cursors/selection disappearing offscreen horizontally or vertically.
- Complex split hierarchies where pane sizes become unmanageable or invisible.
- The need for dynamic gutter widths (e.g., zooming, configurable line‑number width).

---

## 8. Optional advanced path (brief)

If/when you revisit layout more substantially:

1. **Centralize viewport sizing through `geometry.rs`:**
   - Expose helpers that accept *available rect* + font metrics:
     ```rust
     pub fn visible_lines_for_rect(height: f32, line_height: usize) -> usize { /* ... */ }
     pub fn visible_columns_for_rect(width: f32, char_width: f32) -> usize { /* uses text_start_x */ }
     ```
   - Have `sync_all_viewports` call these instead of inline math.

2. **Implement `min_sizes` properly:**
   - In splitter drag logic, adjust `ratios` subject to:
     - `child_size[i] >= min_sizes[i]`
     - `sum(child_size[i]) == total_size`
   - Add tests for min‑size enforcement with 2, 3, and N splits.

3. **Gradually remove `#[allow(dead_code)]`:**
   - Once geometry helpers are wired into both view and input handling, most of them will be used; any leftovers can be safely pruned.

For now, the **only change that clearly deserves action is the `gutter_width` fix**; everything else is either future work or noise.

{
  "path": "~/code/rust-editor/src/view/helpers.rs",
  "content": "//! Shared helper functions for the view layer.\n//!\n//! This module contains utility functions used across multiple view components\n//! to avoid code duplication.\n\nuse token::model::editor_area::Tab;\nuse token::model::AppModel;\n\n/// Get the display title for a tab.\n///\n/// Centralizes the logic for determining what text to show in the tab bar.\n/// Returns \"Untitled\" if the editor or document cannot be found.\npub fn get_tab_display_name(model: &AppModel, tab: &Tab) -> String {\n    model\n        .editor_area\n        .editors\n        .get(&tab.editor_id)\n        .and_then(|e| e.document_id)\n        .and_then(|doc_id| model.editor_area.documents.get(&doc_id))\n        .map(|d| d.display_name())\n        .unwrap_or_else(|| \"Untitled\".to_string())\n}\n\n/// Trim trailing newline from a line of text.\n///\n/// Used for display purposes to avoid rendering the newline character.\n#[inline]\npub fn trim_line_ending(text: &str) -> &str {\n    text.strip_suffix('\\n').unwrap_or(text)\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n\n    #[test]\n    fn test_trim_line_ending_with_newline() {\n        assert_eq!(trim_line_ending(\"hello\\n\"), \"hello\");\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn test_trim_line_ending_without_newline() {\n        assert_eq!(trim_line_ending(\"hello\"), \"hello\");\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn test_trim_line_ending_empty() {\n        assert_eq!(trim_line_ending(\"\"), \"\");\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn test_trim_line_ending_only_newline() {\n        assert_eq!(trim_line_ending(\"\\n\"), \"\");\n    }\n}\n"
}
```

The closure receives\n+    /// the cursor index and should perform the movement for that cursor.\n+    fn for_each_cursor<F>(&mut self, mut f: F)\n+    where\n+        F: FnMut(&mut Self, usize),\n+    {\n+        for i in 0..self.cursors.len() {\n+            f(self, i);\n+        }\n+        self.deduplicate_cursors();\n+    }\n+\n+    /// Apply a cursor movement operation to all cursors and extend selections.\n+    ///\n+    /// After moving each cursor, updates the selection head to the new position.\n+    fn for_each_cursor_extend_selection<F>(&mut self, mut f: F)\n+    where\n+        F: FnMut(&mut Self, usize),\n+    {\n+        for i in 0..self.cursors.len() {\n+            f(self, i);\n+            let pos = self.cursors[i].to_position();\n+            self.selections[i].head = pos;\n+        }\n+        self.deduplicate_cursors();\n+    }\n+\n     /// Move all cursors left\n     /// If a cursor has a selection, collapse to selection start instead of moving by 1 char\n     pub fn move_all_cursors_left(&mut self, doc: &Document) {\n         let len = self.cursors.len();\n```",

This module provides a single source of truth for layout calculations,\n4: //! coordinate transformations, and hit-testing that is shared between\n5: //! the view (rendering) and runtime (input handling) layers.\n6: //!\n7: //! All functions here are pure (no I/O, no side effects) and can be\n8: //! tested independently of the rendering infrastructure.\n9: \n10: use token::model::editor_area::{EditorGroup, Rect};\n11: use token::model::AppModel;\n12: \n13: // ============================================================================\n14: // Layout Constants\n15: // ============================================================================\n16: \n17: /// Height of the tab bar in pixels\n18: pub const TAB_BAR_HEIGHT: usize = 28;\n19: \n20: // Re-export TABULATOR_WIDTH from util::text for single source of truth\n21: pub use token::util::text::TABULATOR_WIDTH;\n22: \n23: // Re-export model geometry helpers for unified access\n24: pub use token::model::{gutter_border_x, text_start_x};\n25: \n26: // ============================================================================\n27: // Viewport Sizing Helpers\n28: // ============================================================================\n29: \n30: /// Calculate the height of the status bar in pixels\n31: #[inline]\n32: pub fn status_bar_height(line_height: usize) -> usize {\n33:     line_height\n34: }\n35: \n36: /// Compute number of visible text lines given window height\n37: #[allow(dead_code)]\n38: pub fn compute_visible_lines(window_height: u32, line_height: usize, status_bar_h: usize) -> usize {\n39:     if line_height == 0 {\n40:         return 25;
